## Inputs (read in full before writing anything)

  `scenes/set.js` (ONE PLACES table — D2), `tools/audit_staging.mjs`.

## Rulings already on record (do not re-decide)
- Qwen conflict 2: the SH14 distant wall is staged as Qwen's 18 D
  seawall-kind silhouette at 140 D on the hill's horizon — ACCEPTED
  by Layla. (Use a film prop entry of the existing `seawall` kind; do
  not author a new kind for it.)
- Qwen conflict 1: word keys stay symbolic (`line:NN.wK`); states hold
  across the plausible word window.

  calendar merges by id, never doubled.
- Hydrogen conflict 1 (SH21 child rig): check `scenes/cast.js` for the

- Hydrogen conflict 2 (seawall_hero): merge `seawall_hero_keys_ext`

  (Law 1); check the h 17.0→18.0 rise against SH05's gate-rise
  staging (no double-driving of the same key).
- Hydrogen conflict 3 (NEIGHBOR_COAST z): read set.js; if neighbour
  walls sit at dz −16, shift all SH23 z offsets by −16 uniformly;
  if not, do not shift. State the evidence.

## The two reconciliations (the heart of this brief)
1. **ONE wave kind.** Qwen's SH18/SH19 wave keys use `h` + `advance`;
   Hydrogen's SH23/SH24 wave uses `crestY` + `curl`. Author ONE
   `scenes/props/wave.js` whose key set serves BOTH (a superset key
   schema is fine if each shot's declared keys drive it
   deterministically). Hard story facts: the wave crest caps at
   17.5 D — strictly below the seawall's 18 D top (the thesis frame
   SH24 keeps a 0.5 D dry-concrete margin). If the two key sets are
   irreconcilable in one kind, STOP on this item and report the
   measured conflict — do NOT silently fork the kind.
2. **ONE gravestone kind.** Qwen: 0.9×1.5×0.25 slab, 4° back lean, no
   keys. Hydrogen SH26 passes `h: 1.35`. Author ONE
   `scenes/props/gravestone.js` (default height 1.5; an `h` entry
   value overrides) serving both.

## THE SPEC — what the gate must do

Input: fleet-probed stills in `probes/chunk2d/stills/frame_NNNN.png`
(1080×1920 PNG, 19 key frames; CLI options `--stills <dir>` and
`--frames N,N` for partial re-probes). Scene data: `scenes/film.js` via the
same loader pipeline the staging audit uses (`lib/film/resolve.js`:
resolver/resolveTimes/autoMove; `lib/film/end.js`: lowCams) — import them,
do not re-implement the loader. Key moments (frame, t, shot):
219/7.30/SH04, 241/8.03/SH04, 288/9.60/SH05, 345/11.50/SH05, 414/13.80/SH05,
474/15.80/SH06, 537/17.90/SH07, 570/19.00/SH07, 590/19.67/SH07B, 621/20.70/SH08,
639/21.30/SH08, 669/22.30/SH09, 687/22.90/SH09, 711/23.70/SH10, 723/24.10/SH10,
768/25.60/SH11, 798/26.60/SH11, 822/27.40/SH11, 870/29.00/SH12.

Three tests, all printed per frame with their evidence:
(a) HEAD CROP — for the required faces (F219/F241: mayor; F537/F570:
mocker_a; F669/F687: mayor; F621/F639: a face pair among mocker_a/mocker_b):
the rendered head disc is MEASURED in the still (skin segmentation ringed by
the characters' dark outline — the driver's reference uses Python PIL +
numpy + scipy, which are installed on this VM; do not add npm deps), matched
to the audit-projected centre/size, and FAILS if the disc is cut by a frame
edge (skin touching an edge, or the expanded disc crossing an edge by
>24 px), if no disc matches, or if the match is ambiguous (two candidates
within tolerance). Calibration (measured on the chunk2d stills): rendered
head radius = 1.04–1.06× the audit r=0.5 sphere for the mayor; ~1.32×
hat-inclusive for villagers/mocker (face-only ~1.10×); rendered centres sit
~340–620 px ABOVE the audit projection on close shots — hence discs are
measured, not projected. Match tolerance: centre distance ≤780 px, disc
size ratio 0.45–2.4 vs the projected diameter, ambiguity = FAIL.
(b) FOREGROUND OCCLUSION — screen-space: a named prop's projected
legibility rect fails if a CLOSER character disc (radius 1.35× the model
sphere) or a closer prop box covers >10% of it. Moments: F345/F414
calendar vs crane_a/crane_b; F711/F723 blueprint vs any cast disc;
F870 mocksign vs any cast disc. Also report a measured-disc overlap (skin
discs vs the prop rect) alongside the geometric overlap.
(c) SET BAR — RULES §5 detail/flat computed on the pixels (32×32 blocks,
grayscale std: flat = std < 5, detail = std ≥ 12), judged per SHOT as §5
writes it (median of the shot's key frames ≥22% detail / ≤78% flat); print
a film-scope line (median shot detail vs the 28% bar; shots under vs 40%).

Output: one line per frame (`F<n> | <shot> | PASS|FAIL | <checks>`) plus
the set-bar line; exit code 1 if any frame fails. Node entry
`tools/audit_pixels.mjs`; the Python measurer `tools/pixel_measure.py`
prints one JSON object per still (flat_pct, detail_pct, discs with skin
bbox / expanded disc bbox / centre / area / dark-adjacency).

## SPEC CORRECTIONS — Astra review (Miora, GPT-6 Astra), 2026-11-23, batches 1+2 — GOVERNING

A review seat (GPT-6 Astra) audited the driver's reference implementation
and found real defects. Your re-derivation must FIX all four; a version
that reproduces the validation table while keeping any of them is a
FAILED delivery.

1. **DEAD EDGE-CROSS CHECK (batch 1).** The reference `pixel_measure.py`
   CLAMPS the expanded disc bounds to the image, so the "disc crosses an
   edge by >24 px" condition in test (a) can NEVER trigger — crop
   verdicts currently rest on the skin-touch test alone. Your version
   must preserve UNCLIPPED expanded bounds through the JSON and evaluate
   the cross condition on them.
2. **PAIR DETECTION MUST MATCH BOTH CHARACTERS (batch 1).** The
   reference passes the F621/F639 face-pair moment on ONE large segmented
   region. Your version passes a pair moment only when BOTH named
   characters' discs are individually matched (each within tolerance of
   its own projection); one region, however large, is not a pair.
3. **OCCLUSION NEEDS A DEPTH TEST (batch 1).** The reference's
   measured-disc overlap fails a prop rect when ANY disc overlaps it,
   including unmatched/background discs — contradicting the spec's
   "closer occluder" requirement. Your version counts a measured disc as
   an occluder only with depth evidence it is closer than the prop
   (e.g. matched to a cast member whose projected depth is nearer than
   the prop's, or demonstrably larger/nearer by the calibrated disc
   model); background or unmatched discs are reported, not counted.
4. **MEASUREMENT GEOMETRY — CLUSTER BOUNDS, SAMPLED STATE (batch 2,
   from the F590 cause).** Wherever a subject's size is measured for an
   occupancy-style judgement, measure the RENDERED CLUSTER'S AGGREGATE
   BOUNDS (the union of its members' bounds), computed from the SAMPLED
   EASED CAMERA and the prop's STATE AT t (e.g. the coins' drop state),
   never the prop's 0.5-unit origin proxy box and never the authored TO
   pose alone. (The same correction is owed to the staging audit's
   occupancy test — a separate seat session, recorded in RUN_RECORD;
   your scope stays the two files below, but do not reintroduce the
   proxy error anywhere in them.) Also verify any reported cluster
   bounds hold meaningful content, not empty spacing between members.

- SH07's F570 and F590 requirements pull the camera to opposite sides of a depth constraint. The mocker head `[4,1.9,0]` and wall-top `[3,18,-16]` need a village-side low camera near `[4.1,0.2,1.6]` for a shared narrow frustum. From that region, the pinned coin at about `[2.1,19.73,-17.5]` is roughly 19 depth units away; at fov 24 the audit's 230px occupancy threshold requires depth about 11 or less (the 0.5m coin box projects only about 75–81px in the tested F590 village-side samples). The F590 passing reference camera had z=-11.74, on the seawall/coin side; a straight camera interpolation cannot move from z≈+1.6 at F570 to that near coin-side position by F590 while also keeping F537's face close-up. Do not move the coin drop or alter audit occupancy to conceal this conflict.
- Verified result for this iteration: SH11 passes all three probes; SH07 fails F537, F570 and F590 in the final camera attempt. `audit_d1_d2.mjs` remains PASS. The constrained SH07 camera requirements need an authorized change to the one-move camera model or one of the pinned checks before this audit can reach 19/19.

## Phase-8 lessons — 2026-11-23 (SH07 line-6 split attempt)
- Restore the SH07 baseline endpoints before splitting: close-up from `CAM_SH07_A` / `CAM_SH07_TA`, and the coin-side finish at `CAM_SH07_B` / `CAM_SH07_TB`, with fov 50→24 and roll -5→-2. SH11 stays at its passing park.
- A split on `line:6.word:9` (19.17 s, between words 8 and 10) can separate the mockery close-up from the coin-side move. The padded camera interval changes the sample fraction: inspect the audit's printed CAM rows after every ease change. `inExpo` kept F537's face in frame in this attempt.
- The current audit hard-codes F537, F570 and F590 to `shotById('SH07')`, and `shotById` returns the first matching shot by id. After adding SH07B, F590 is still measured on SH07 at its end pose; it never samples SH07B. The resulting F590 CAM row `[5.50, 1.80, 4.20]`, fov 50 and 43 px coins is evidence that the split's second shot is not bound. Correct time-to-shot lookup is needed before the audit can judge the split as intended.
- This attempt did not reach 19/19: F537 passed, while F570's head sphere and wall-top point remained outside the frustum and F590 remained bound to SH07. Do not report a passing split from a run where the sampled shot id does not match the moment's active interval.

## Phase-8 lesson — SH07 split binding and F570 occlusion limit (2026-11-23)
- A split shot needs every sampled audit moment rebound to the shot ID active at that time. `shotById` returns the first matching ID, so leaving F590 bound to `SH07` silently audits the pull-back instead of `SH07B`; rebind the F590 moment to `SH07B` when the split is introduced.
- With that binding, SH07B can pass F590 by lifting its camera above the seawall and aiming between the wall-top point and coin drop. The measured passing F590 camera is `[2.51,18.71,-10.51]`, target `[1.86,17.76,-32.25]`, fov `31.19`; coins are 254px and the wall-top point is in-frame.
- F570's fixed head and wall-top checks conflict under the current seawall occluder model: from the village side the ray to `[3,18,-16]` intersects the seawall; from the sea side the mocker head cannot meet the 230px occupancy threshold. Do not claim 19/19 or change audit checks under a camera-only split brief; this requires an owner-authorized audit/geometry decision.

## Phase-8 lesson — visible wall-top test point (2026-11-23)
- A test point must be a point the render can show. A point inside the tested mesh's own box is self-occluded; test the visible edge. This is the same family as the sea-span measure correction.

## Phase-8 lesson — spread stacked cast (2026-11-23)
- Stacked cast at one point is a render fault the staging audit cannot see: a character can merge with another or occlude a face while all audit moments still pass. Check cast spreads with the hunter and fleet stills, alongside the staging audit.

## Phase 9 — METHOD CHANGE #3: the pixel-verified loop (2026-11-23)
- Critic round 8 (precision round, sort 0·0, VERDICT: NO) named the same fault family a second time — cropped heads, occluded props, missing subjects, foreign sign text — while the scene-data staging audit read 19/19 PASS. Per RULES §3 the METHOD changed: `tools/audit_pixels.mjs` (+ `tools/pixel_measure.py`) now judges the fleet-probed STILLS. The scene-data audit is a pre-check only; **no frame is called staged until the PIXEL gate passes on its probed still.**
- The pixel gate's three tests: (a) HEAD CROP — the required face's rendered head disc is MEASURED in the still (skin segmentation ringed by the dark outline, scipy components), matched to the projected centre/size, and fails if edge-cut, absent, or ambiguous; (b) FOREGROUND OCCLUSION — screen-space: a named prop's projected legibility rect fails if a closer character disc (calibrated radius) or a closer prop box covers >10% of it (the staging audit's occluder set — buildings + wall + gateleaf — cannot see character-on-prop or prop-on-prop occlusion; at F345/F414 the crane_b box overlaps the calendar's rect 100%); (c) SET BAR — §5 detail/flat measured on the pixels (32×32 blocks, grayscale std; flat = std<5, detail = std≥12), judged per SHOT as §5 writes it (median of the shot's key frames ≥22% detail / ≤78% flat; film median shot ≥28%; ≤40% of shots under).
- CALIBRATION (measured on the chunk2d stills, 2026-11-23): rendered head radius vs the audit's r=0.5 sphere — mayor ≈1.04–1.06× (F669: R 431 vs 414; F687: R 473 vs 448, disc centre (553,30) solved from the chord at y=0 + chin at y≈503); hat-wearing villager/mocker silhouette ≈1.32× (F537: 648×624 px vs the 480 px model disc; face-only ≈1.10×). Occlusion discs use k=1.35 (worst case, hat-inclusive).
- CALIBRATION — centre offsets: the rendered head centre sits far ABOVE the audit's projected centre on close shots (F687 ≈ −623 px, F669 ≈ −576 px, F537 ≈ −342 px vertically). The audit's camera model (approximated lowCams rest; renderer face-limit clamping unmodelled) does not reproduce the render camera's effective aim for close shots — this, not the 5% radius error, is the head-crop mechanism: the sphere test passes a head whose rendered disc is centred ~600 px higher than the model thinks. Hence test (a) measures discs in the image; projected centres are used only as match seeds (tolerance 780 px + size-ratio window 0.45–2.4 + ambiguity = FAIL).
- Gate validation on the unrepaired chunk2d stills: PASS exactly on the frames the critic/driver judged good (F288, F537, F621, F639, F870) and FAIL on every faulted frame, each with its cause named (F219/F241 mayor not identifiable; F345/F414 calendar under crane_b 100%; F474 set 13.7%/85.1%; F570 no disc segments — the back-of-head does not read as skin; F590 set 14.5%/84.4%; F669/F687 skin touches the top edge; F711/F723 blueprint 46% geometric / 74% measured-disc overlap by villager_4; F768/F798/F822 set ~14%/81%). Chunk-scope set bar: median shot detail 26.7% (< 28% film bar), 3/10 shots under.

## Phase 8 — audit-model blind spots (learned from the fleet eye-verdicts, 2026-11-23)
- The staging audit's head sphere (r=0.5) is SMALLER than the rendered head mesh: "head in" passed at F669/F687 while the renders crop the head's top. The audit is a NECESSARY gate, never a sufficient one — fleet stills + the driver's eye + the critic remain the judges.
- The audit does not model FACING/legibility: at F570 the mocker's head passed geometrically (520px) but renders as an illegible back-of-head against the blueprint grid that fills the frame's lower half. A passing subject must also READ.
- Rendered prop clusters can sit outside the audit's prop box: the coins' rendered discs ride ~1 D above the audit's 0.5 D drop box (F590: coins cropped at the frame's top edge, measured y 0–12.4%, while the audit's box read 8/8 corners in). When a prop's visual is a cluster, audit the cluster's bounds, not the origin box.
- Fleet render spec: a single frame is `render:N:N+1` — END must be greater than START (`render:N:N` fails in parseModeSpec). 19 runs were lost to this once; never again.
- Guard flag 3: bundling three one-value fixes into one session is still bundling. One cause, one session, one re-judgement — even when each item is a single constant.

## Phase 10 — CodePen framing lab adopted as a standing pre-check (owner's word, 2026-11-23 ~05:01)
- Owner's word: «اعتمديه في الشغل» — the CodePen framing lab (pen «معمل التأطير — كاميرا ٩:١٦», editor Araby-Mahmoud, pen 01a20657-7257-7466-8fb1-1bdd85dabbd4; preview https://01a20657-7257-7466-8fb1-1bdd85dabbd4.codepen.dev/) is adopted in the work: a 9:16 Three.js frame (wall + character + sign) with camera pos / target-Y / fov sliders, reading live — head fully in frame or cropped, head height in px at 1920, top margin in px.
- STANDING USE: before any fleet re-probe of a re-staged close/medium shot, sanity-check the shot's camera/target/fov values in this lab pattern (head-in-frame + head-px + top-margin readouts) and record the lab readout beside the cause in RUN_RECORD; a lab FAIL blocks the probe dispatch until the staging is corrected. Pre-check only — the pixel gate and the critic remain the judges.
- Driver execution note (phase 10): the driver has no browser; the readout is computed in the lab's exact pattern (the audit's screen() projection + the pixel-calibrated rendered-disc offsets from the phase-9 calibration) and recorded beside the cause. The interactive pen itself is Layla's browser surface.
- KEEPER APPOINTED (owner's word, 2026-11-23 ~05:02 — if the lab needs an agent appointed to it, Layla should not hesitate): **Seat 6 (styles & library keeper) owns the CodePen framing lab** — keeps the pen current, extends it when a new fault class needs a visual pre-check, and its readouts are the ones recorded beside causes.

## Phase 10 lessons — 2026-11-23
- SCREEN-Y RESPONSE SIGN (measured twice, both directions): lowering a camera target pitches the camera down and moves frame content UP (screen y decreases) — the audit model's projected head centre moved −744/−789 px for a −0.915 target change on SH09. Luna's SH09 correction assumed the opposite sign and pushed the rendered head from crown-cut to fully out of frame; it was reverted on the fleet evidence. Compute target changes from the measured slope (≈813–862 px per unit on SH09), never from intuition about "aiming lower".
- PIXEL GATE measured-disc FALSE POSITIVE (driver-harness note for the re-derivation spec): the gate's occlusion test (b) flags any segmented skin-toned disc over a prop's rect — the CALENDAR'S OWN cream page segments as a disc (F345: the calendar page itself read as 100% measured-disc overlap while the eye shows it fully legible and geometric overlap is 0%), and on frames flooded with tan wall/ground the segmentation returns one frame-filling "disc" (F345/F687). Judge test (b) by the geometric overlap + the eye when the named occluder is a prop and the "disc" coincides with the prop's own rendered face.
- Pixel gate (driver-authored reference) — three defects found by Astra's review (2026-11-23, phase 11): (1) pixel_measure.py CLAMPS expanded disc bounds to the image, making audit_pixels' "disc crosses edge by >24px" check dead code — crop verdicts actually rest on the skin-touch test alone; preserve UNCLIPPED bounds for the cross check. (2) Pair detection passes on one large segmented region — it must match BOTH named characters' discs before passing a two-face moment. (3) Measured-disc overlap has no depth test — an occluder must be CLOSER (larger/nearer disc or z evidence), not merely overlapping, or background discs false-fail occlusion. Any re-derivation must fix all three and still reproduce the validation table.
- Projected bbox "frame share" from a camera sampler does NOT account for occlusion: F590 round 2's sea-plane share read 23.8% in projection but ~7% in the render (the wall and village occlude most of the plane). Treat projected shares of large background planes as upper bounds, never as the frame's measured composition; the pixel gate on the still is the composition judge.
- Astra batch-2 method adoptions (2026-11-23): (1) STANDING: approve staging with projected evidence, then approve the result with rendered pixels — neither substitutes for the other. (2) An occlusion explanation must account for EVERY hidden glyph: "5M" visible from "15.5M" means "15." is covered, not just "1" — check the kind's source text first (camera moves cannot repair wrong underlying text), then confirm the full inscription on the post-fix still. (3) A head disc out of frame does not prove palm/arm coverage — check the named body part specifically. (4) Occupancy-style measurements use the rendered cluster's aggregate bounds from the sampled eased camera + state at t, never the origin proxy box or the authored TO pose. (5) Composition root causes to name before computing: inverted subject hierarchy (SH04 — protect the lead's screen region), wrong side of the occluding surface (F474 — establishing beats go village-side oblique), uniform-sky allocation (SH11 — layered roofline low, clear corridor high).
# Production and rules — the operating law of the heart

This folder is the complete Creatorize Suite **0.28.3**, fully extracted (see `COMPLETENESS.md` and
`EXTRACTION.md`). The heart — the main-process core — is at
`suite-win-portable/app.asar.extracted/out/main/index.js`, with its Python pipeline spine at
`suite-win-portable/app.asar.extracted/pipeline-scripts/`. The rules below are distilled **only**
from the extracted files themselves; each rule names its source. Nothing here is imported from
outside the heart.

The flagship method is the cartoon-shorts skill
(`suite-win-portable/resources/templates/skill/cartoon-shorts/SKILL.md`); the other 18 skills carry
the same discipline per style/engine. Where a rule comes from that skill it is cited as *(SKILL)*.

---

## 1. The five passes — order is law

Every film is built in five passes, in this order, each run from its own step file, each standing
alone and keeping what the others made. "End to end" = the five in that order, with the director's
whole-film review between them. *(SKILL, "The stages")*

| # | Pass | Step file | Exit (summary; the full list is in the step file) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Build | `steps/build.md` | Frame 0 an expressive face; every word in a line; PROP GUARD passes; critic YES |
| 2 | Camera angles | `steps/camera.md` | Every fast move clean on a full-rate strip; `film_check same` passes; critic YES |
| 3 | Environment overhaul | `steps/environment.md` | The set bar passes; depth behind every face; critic YES |
| 4 | Add post processing | `steps/post.md` | Numbers per place class; faces the sharpest thing; set bar still passes; critic YES |
| 5 | Shading pass | `steps/shading.md` | `shade_jumps`/`calm` looked at; set bar still passes; critic YES |

Priority inside the work, the owner's words: **characters first** ("they are what makes the whole
thing feel alive"), **then the camera, the set, the post, the light**. *(SKILL, "Your role")*

## 2. How every pass runs — the loop

1. **Read** the step file, its named references, every earlier pass's `lessons/*.md` HANDOVER, the
   bar stills, and the guide pages for this pass (always pages 01 and 10).
2. **Before touching anything:** `render_frames` at `scale: 1` on 4–6 key times + a whole-film
   `film_sheet`. Write the gap list — what the bar has that these frames lack, worst first.
3. **Work:** change something real, `check_preview`, LOOK (`film_sheet`, `strip` on fast moves,
   `render_frames` on faces). Fix the worst thing radically, at its cause. Again.
4. **Machine checks** of the pass: every FAIL fixed at its cause. *Numbers point, eyes decide.*
5. **Critic rounds** (see §3). 6. **Handover:** `lessons/<step>.md`, ending with the HANDOVER block.
*(SKILL, "How every pass runs")*

## 3. Critic discipline — you never grade your own work

- Every pass ends with critic rounds: a **new** agent that never saw your reasoning sees your
  frames **blind-mixed with bar frames** (same size, shuffled, neutral names; the key file is
  never shown to the critic) and answers in a fixed form.
- Read the answer; count the blind score (bar pictures it took for yours / yours it took for the
  bar) and write both numbers in the lessons.
- **Fix only the BIGGEST GAP**, at its cause, radically — one cause, one change. A BLOCKING fault
  the critic marks as a one-line fix may ride along.
- Next round = a **NEW critic**, same moments + the last report.
- **The pass is done when its exit list is true AND a critic answers `VERDICT: YES` with no
  BLOCKING fault.**
- **The same fault twice** means the fix was the wrong kind: change the method, not the amount
  (geometry instead of a texture, a real light instead of a brighter grade, another camera).
- The director's review between passes never replaces critic rounds.
*(SKILL references/critic.md)*

## 4. Hard rules

- **Made new from this voice:** cast, acting, shots, camera and set are written for this film.
  Examples teach the method and set the bar; they are never copied as material.
- **Open on a character's face**, scared or expressive, in the first frame. Never an empty scene,
  never a back.
- **Pure in t:** every frame is a function of t alone. No `Math.random` (use a seeded generator),
  no `Date`, no state between frames.
- **Never edit the engine copy** (`engine/`, `world/`, `lib/`, `specs/`). New looks, sets, props
  and characters go in `scenes/`. If an engine change seems needed, work around it and record it
  in the lessons as a gap.
- **Never touch `audio/`.** Voice lines are written as heard.
- **Anything in a place is in every shot of it.** Faces read: within about 60° of the camera, big,
  never cut by the edge.
- **PROP GUARD:** a prop that jumps on screen fails the film. `pick_up`, `put_down`, hand-overs,
  place changes on cuts, `snap` on real hits.
- **Later passes never change what earlier passes made** unless that is their job.
- Stay inside the project. Pictures only through `make_picture`; never a paid service the member
  did not choose.
*(SKILL, "Hard rules")*

## 5. The set bar — measured, and export refuses below it

`film_check { kind: 'set' }`, measured by the app: a shot needs **≥ 22 % detailed set** and
**≤ 80 % flat**; the film needs a **median shot of ≥ 28 % detail** and **≤ 78 % flat**, with
**no more than 40 % of its shots under**. Environment, Post and Shading cannot finish below it,
and **the app's export calls a film below it unfinished**. *(SKILL, "Hard rules")*

## 6. Quality has no clock

"A pass is done when its exit list is true AND a fresh critic says YES. Never cut a beat, a
character, a set piece or an effect to finish; never turn an effect down until it is gone to make
a problem disappear." Only the app's FASTER BUILDS block, when the member turned it on, speaks
about time. *(SKILL, "Quality first")* The bar is the LOOK, never the subject: any topic reaches
the same depth, light, air and acting with its own places. *(SKILL, "The bar")*

## 7. Voice and words

- The voice is law: `audio/clean.wav` + `audio/clean.json` (the trimmed voice and its word times)
  are **made by the app: never edit**. *(SKILL, project table)*
- Audio step 1 — tighten pauses without damaging the voice (`pipeline-scripts/audio_prep.py`):
  a pause = RMS below **−50 dB** for ≥ 0.12 s; word tails/onsets found at **−62 dB** with a 25 ms
  guard each side; only the middle of the silence is removed, each pause keeps **110–200 ms** so
  sentence rhythm survives; every join gets a **20 ms equal-power crossfade**; peak-normalised to
  **−1 dBFS**; output 48 kHz mono + a cuts report with a click test at every join.
- Step 2 — transcribe (`transcribe.py`): the raw transcript is only a draft; a human/agent reads
  it and fixes proper nouns, numbers and homophones into `audio/transcript.txt` — **align.py
  force-aligns that exact text, so what is written there is what the timings and captions
  contain.**
- Step 3 — align (`align.py`): forced alignment → snap to waveform → pause anchoring (every real
  pause, < −50 dB ≥ 90 ms, must fall BETWEEN words) → phrases from the audio's own voiced regions
  → an independent per-phrase re-transcription check → false-pause merging. Outputs
  `audio/timeline.json`, `audio/script_timed.txt` (the storyboard source), `audio/captions.srt`,
  `audio/align_report.json`.

## 8. Sound design and the mix — pass criteria

- Beds/SFX are **synthesized procedurally, no samples, no licensing** (`sound_design.py`):
  beds high-passed at **40 Hz**, soft-limited at **−20 dBFS**; noise beds low-passed; no tonal
  whines above ~1 kHz; **2.5 s crossfades** between beds; bed gains are target RMS **−29…−32 dBFS**
  (the tasteful range) before the −4 dB global trim.
- Lanes (`lanes_mix.py`): every clip on its lane (trimmed, looped, faded, panned, levelled,
  **ducked under the voice**), per-lane EQ + glue compression, one shared room reverb, effects
  bus through a gentle limiter; voice-activity for ducking is measured in 10 ms blocks at
  −45…−30 dBFS.
- Final mix (`mix.py`) **pass criteria, printed in `audio/mix_report.json`**: voice-to-bed ratio
  while speaking **≥ 16 dB** (`--min-ratio`; style variants: stick-story 12, because its
  references sit 10–13 LU under the voice); **true peak ≤ −1.0 dB** (mix step's own ceiling
  default −1.5 dB; low-poly −2 dB); **integrated loudness within 0.5 LU of target**, target
  default **−16 LUFS** (loudnorm LRA = 11). If the ratio is too low: **lower the gains in
  beds.json and re-run sound_design — do not touch the voice.** Bed duck sidechain default 3.0
  (low-poly 1.5, effects stay audible). The user's own music is first levelled to −16 LUFS so its
  dB setting means the same for any track, then ducked (default 8 dB) under the voice.

## 9. Project anatomy

A film project is data a page renders at time t — you write files; the app previews, renders and
exports. *(SKILL, "The project")*

| Path | Law |
|---|---|
| `scenes/BIBLE.md` | The film bible — Build writes it first: lines, beats, places with sizes in D, cast, story props, the colour script |
| `scenes/film.js` | THE film: `export default { … }` — cast, voice lines, acts, props, shots, captions, world |
| `scenes/set.js` | The film's own set: `export async function buildSet(THREE, scene, opts, kit)` |
| `scenes/props/<kind>.js`, `scenes/cast/<id>.json` | The film's own props and characters |
| `scenes/post.js`, `scenes/shade.js` | Post and shading settings as data, imported by film.js |
| `assets/set/` | Blender output: `<place>.glb`, `kit.glb`, `scatter.json` |
| `assets/tex/` | Textures, `decals.png`, `tex.json`, `recipes.json`, `raw/`, `sprites/` |
| `audio/` | The voice and its products — never edited by hand (§7) |
| `lessons/<step>.md` | What each pass learned, ending with its HANDOVER block — read first when any pass runs |
| `.suite/critic/` | The critic rounds: the pictures each critic saw and its reports |
| engine copy (`engine.js`, `engine/`, `vendor/`, `specs/`, `src/`, `tracks/`, `world/`, `lib/`) | Read and import — **never edit** |

Scaffolding (`pipeline-scripts/init_project.py`): a project carries its own `engine.js`,
`models.js`, `index.html`, vendored `three.module.js` and fonts — **renders never depend on a
CDN** — plus `project.json` (scene list, images, end time), `audio/source.<ext>`, `scenes/`,
`assets/`, `out/`.

## 10. Film format

- Cartoon-shorts (Three.js engine): **`fps: 30`**, `size: [1080, 1920]` (9:16) or `[1920, 1080]`
  in a wide project; the project's format decides. *(skill/cartoon-shorts/references/film.md)*
- Roblox-complex engine: `fps: 60`, `size: [1080, 1440]`, library pin `cxlib-3`
  (699,311,790 B, SHA-256 `ad112883…e672d`, 1,218 files). *(resources/templates/robloxcx/VERSION.json)*

## 11. The styles system

A style = a **skill** (the full method: SKILL.md + `steps/` + `references/` + `tools/`) + its
**template folders** (29 under `resources/templates/`) + an engine. The 19 skills held in
`resources/templates/skill/`: 3d-documentary · blender-toon-shorts · cartoon-shorts ·
flat-explainer-video · intro-edit · lowpoly-shorts · mega-informational · pixel-explainer-video ·
recon-reconstruction · roblox-complex · roblox-lightweight · roblox-story-shorts ·
stickman-explainer · stickstory-videos · studio-starter · talking-head-edit · toon-shorts ·
vox-collage-video · vox-editorial-video. Each skill's own SKILL.md is that style's law (e.g.
low-poly: *one clause = one shot, a camera that always moves*; blender-toon: 49 expression
presets, automatic lip-sync, sitting checked every frame).

## 12. Integrity, the gate, and the one door — the core's own constitution

From `out/main/index.js` (the heart; line anchors from the 2026-11-22 core review):

- **Integrity:** `resources/files.json` is a per-file SHA-256 manifest of the whole install
  (0.28.3 · win32 · 8,083 files); the installer's repair mode re-verifies every installed file
  against it.
- **The gate never touches the member's password:** it loads Skool's own page in a hidden
  sandboxed window (`persist:skool`) and reads membership state from the page's `__NEXT_DATA__`
  (role, group, `mmbp` tier → plan). The saved pass is sealed with OS-keychain `safeStorage`
  (HMAC-signed fallback); grace is exactly **12 h**; rechecks run every 30–50 min; a definitive
  not-a-member verdict clears the pass instantly — but **the lock waits for in-flight
  exports/builds and never cuts a render**. Even the maker's own patch channel is code-barred
  from gate/license files (`NEVER_PATCH`).
- **Callers:** all logic lives in the main process (≈521 functional IPC channels across 60
  domains); the channel helper rejects any sender that is not the app's own main window
  ("IPC from an untrusted frame"). There is **no CLI/headless entry**.
- **The one external door:** an MCP bridge at `127.0.0.1:47913` — loopback-Host-only,
  Origin-checked, POST-only, ~80 suite tools, advertised for the member's own Claude Code — and
  it starts **only after the gate passes**.
- **State:** a 26-table `suite.db` (projects, renders, agent threads, learning library, rules,
  YouTube analytics); every project folder carries git as its undo history; pipeline-scripts are
  self-healing (installed → healed copy in userData → bundled spares).

## 13. Tools and failure law

The pass tools: `check_preview`, `render_frames`, `film_sheet`, `film_probe`,
`film_check { set|same|calm|flicker|shade_jumps }`, `list_shots`, `narration`, `blender_run`,
`make_picture`, `tex_job`, `prop_library`. Every failure starts `TOOL PROBLEM [<code>]`;
`references/tools-sop.md` says what each code means and what to do — **fix or fetch, retry,
never give up on something fixable**. In Bash the runtime is `$CZ_PYTHON`, `$CZ_FFMPEG`,
`$CZ_FFPROBE`, `$CZ_BLENDER`, `$CZ_NODE`. *(SKILL, "Tools")*

## 14. Runtime law — the app fetches its own, hash-checked

On first run the app downloads and **hash-checks its own ffmpeg 9.0.2 and Python 3.12**,
ignoring system copies (the `PINS` table in `out/main/index.js`): 21 pinned artifacts —
ffmpeg per platform + ffprobe extras, the Python embed/cpython builds, numpy/scipy/pillow
wheels, the MSVC runtime wheel, pip 25.3 — all held extracted under `runtime-pins/` here.
Blender **5.2.3** is fetched on first use of a Blender-engine style from download.blender.org
(+ 5 mirrors), per the bundle's BUILDS table: win32-x64, win32-arm64, darwin-arm64 (there is
**no darwin-x64 build** — the app itself says Blender 5.2 runs only on Apple silicon). Held
extracted under `blender/`. Setup law (`pipeline-scripts/setup.py`): system tools are ffmpeg
(+ffprobe), Node ≥ 18, Chrome/Edge/Chromium; Python packages numpy, pillow, scipy, stable-ts
(whisper + torch); installing system software is left to the user/agent to confirm.

files, it is not in this document.*
# CRITIC PROTOCOL — Fudai Wall (Seat 3, internal fresh critic)

Project: `fudai-wall` · Film: the mayor's 15.5 m seawall (68.08 s, vertical 9:16)
Seat: **Seat 3 — internal fresh critic**, Layla's office. Reports go to the production driver
(coordinator) and into the run record — never to the owner directly.
Law: `~/workspace/Production and rules/RULES.md` §3 (critic discipline), §4 (hard rules),
§5 (set bar), §7 (voice is law). Sources of truth for content: the owner's scene bible
`~/workspace/user/files/Fudai_Wall_Scene_Visualization_22_7j0z.txt` and his word timestamps
`~/workspace/user/files/Fudai_Wall_Word_Timestamps_21_v6r1.txt` (186 words, 00:00.10–01:07.55).

## 1. How I work (RULES §3 — non-negotiable)

- I am **fresh**: I never saw the build seats' reasoning, code, or intentions. I judge only
  what I am shown. I do not read the driver's gap list before judging.
- I receive the board/probe frames **blind-mixed with bar frames**: same size, shuffled,
  neutral names. The key file is never shown to me. I first sort every picture as
  "ours" or "bar", then judge ours against the bible line by line.
- I answer in the **fixed form** (§5 below). The **BIGGEST GAP** is named first, at its cause.
- The pass is done only when its exit list is true **AND** I answer `VERDICT: YES` with no
  BLOCKING fault. A `VERDICT: NO` blocks the pass: no checkpoint, no export, no exceptions.
- After a NO, the driver fixes **only the biggest gap**, radically, at its cause (one cause,
  one change). A BLOCKING fault I mark as a one-line fix may ride along.
- Next round = a **new critic**, same moments + my last report (§3). This sheet is the
  continuity artifact; my reports accumulate in `.suite/critic/` beside the pictures I saw.
- The same fault twice means the fix was the wrong kind — I say so and name the method change
  (geometry instead of texture, a real light instead of a brighter grade, another camera).
- The director's review never replaces critic rounds. I never grade my own work: I build
  nothing on this project.

## 2. Standing BLOCKING checks (the prior run's failure modes — automatic NO)

These failed the earlier run and the critic's NO was right both times. Any one of them,
anywhere on the board, is BLOCKING → `VERDICT: NO`, no further scoring needed:

1. **Empty probe** — a frame of sky/sand/sea/caption only, with the scene's subject absent
   (the camera-vs-set-coordinates defect). A board still must contain its scene.
2. **Missing named prop at zoom** — any prop the bible names for that moment not visibly
   present and framed (prop-truth): the 15.5M gate, the 5-storey overlay, the 3,000 sign,
   the $30M piles, the calendar, the 10M labels, the blueprint + ruler, the grave, the rock
   carving, the pencil tick.
3. **Gutted scene** — a scene present as backdrop only, with its cast/beat absent.
4. **Frame 0 not a face** — the film must open on a character's expressive face; never an
   empty scene, never a back (§4).

## 3. What I judge at the board pass

### 3.1 Blind sort (RULES §3)
Sort the shuffled set into ours/bar. Report both blind numbers: bar pictures I took for
ours / ours I took for the bar. Both numbers go into the lessons/run record verbatim.

### 3.2 Per-scene fidelity to the owner's bible
Each scene PASS/FAIL against its bible beats (all beats of the scene's window must read
in the stills chosen for that window):

- **S1 · 00:00.10–00:06.56 — the hook.** Giant grey concrete wall; transparent 5-storey
  building overlay numbered 1–5 beside it; the mayor small at its base; fishing village
  behind the wall reading as a miniature; mirror-calm sea; one still fishing boat.
- **S2 · 00:07.06–00:16.77 — the bet in numbers.** Mayor in the village square among
  villagers, floating «3,000» sign; construction time-lapse: cranes, cartoon money piles
  «$30M», fast-flipping calendar (12 years), the steel-concrete gate rising, glowing
  measure arrow reaching «15.5M»; split comparison: neighbouring walls labelled «10M».
- **S3 · 00:17.28–00:24.52 — mockery vs silence.** Big close-up (slight Dutch angle) of a
  laughing villager pointing at the wall top, then the calm sea; imagined coins falling
  into water; deadpan mayor close-up — mouth closed, cold calm gaze; blueprint insert: a
  hand pulls the height line down, the mayor's hand pins the ruler at 15.5M.
- **S4 · 00:25.09–00:35.27 — the long wait.** Completed wall + calm sea under cycling
  sun/moon/seasons, not one wave; mocking wooden sign («أغبى مشروع»); white-haired retired
  mayor leaving the municipality carrying his box, accusing fingers behind him; quiet hill
  grave overlooking the silent wall, one leaf falling, blue-gold melancholic light.
- **S5 · 00:35.70–00:44.81 — the wave.** Calendar flip (14 years), sky snapping to stormy
  blue-grey; violent shake — swaying poles, fleeing birds, wide-eyed villagers; the sea
  receded, boats stranded on exposed sand; a huge dark wall of water, glowing measure
  rising to exactly «15M», people running uphill.
- **S6 · 00:45.45–00:57.57 — the memory.** Warm sepia flashback: young black-haired mayor
  watching old waves swallow houses; close tracking along the mountain rock with its carved
  horizontal line, his young hand touching the groove; clean cutaway: warm room wall, a
  pencil tick drawn above a cartoon child's head with the measure written beside it;
  match-cut: the pencil tick becomes the top edge of the concrete wall, workers pouring
  the last metre.
- **S7 · 00:58.00–01:07.55 — the payoff.** Aerial: neighbouring 10M towns overtopped and
  wiped in minutes; side profile: the wave slams the 15.5M wall, spray + shake, water
  rising to **exactly the top edge — and stopping**; crane over the wall: the village dry
  and intact under a warm sunbeam; the same mockers of S3, silent and grateful with flowers
  at the mayor's grave, the giant wall behind them; hard cut on the last word (loop cut).

For every spoken line window (owner's timestamps), the visual active in that window must
be the bible beat for that line — no beat early, no beat late, no line over the wrong image:

Numbers are story facts and must read exactly: **15.5 m** gate / **10 m** neighbours /
**15 m** wave / **3,000** people / **$30M** / **12 years** building / **14 years** after his
death. A wrong or missing number on screen is BLOCKING.

### 3.4 Identity and look (owner's visual bible)
- Characters are the bible's 2D flat design: big round head, expressive dot eyes, bean body,
  thin arms/legs, thick black outline. The mayor: short grey hair, dark municipal suit,
  upright, calm fixed gaze (white hair after retirement; black hair in the S6 flashback).
  Villagers: warm contrasting fisher clothes and hats; mockery exaggerated in S3, shock and
  gratitude later — the S7 mourners must be recognisably the S3 mockers.
- Lighting arc: warm low gold sun with cool blue-violet shade → stormy dramatic blue-grey
  (S5) → warm golden dawn (S7). A scene lit against its arc is a gap.
- Faces read: within ~60° of camera, big, never cut by the edge (§4). Characters first,
  then camera, set, post, light (§1 priority).

### 3.5 Hard rules and the set bar (RULES §4–§5)
- **PROP GUARD:** no prop jumps, teleports, or changes place across a cut inside its place;
  anything in a place is in every shot of that place.
- **Set bar (measured by `film_check {kind:'set'}`; numbers point, eyes decide):** every
  shot ≥ 22 % detailed set and ≤ 80 % flat; film median ≥ 28 % detail and ≤ 78 % flat; no
  more than 40 % of shots under the bar. A board below the bar cannot pass, and export
  refuses it — so do I.
- Depth behind every face (pass-3 exit, checked early at the board): a face against flat
  nothing is a gap even in a Build-pass board.

## 4. What I need from the driver to judge

1. The board stills at the bible's key moments (at minimum one per beat in §3.2, plus the
   zoom prop frames), **blind-mixed with bar frames** of the same moments, same size,
   shuffled, neutral file names. The key stays with the driver.
2. The `film_check {kind:'set'}` numbers for the board's shots.
3. Nothing else — no intentions, no explanations, no code. Explanations after my verdict
   are the lessons' job, not the round's.

## 5. Fixed answer form (verbatim structure, filed in `.suite/critic/` and the run record)

```
CRITIC ROUND — fudai-wall — <pass name> — round <n>
BLIND SORT: bar taken for ours: <n> · ours taken for bar: <n>
BIGGEST GAP: <one cause, named first>
BLOCKING: <list, or "none">
PER SCENE: S1 … S7 — PASS/FAIL each, one line each
LINE COHERENCE: <windows that mismatch, or "all windows match">
SET BAR: <numbers as given> — above/below
VERDICT: YES | VERDICT: NO
```

Filed 2026-11-22 (Seat 3 standing appointment) before the board exists: `out/board/` is
empty, `.suite/critic/` held no prior reports for this run. I am standing by; when the
board stills land I judge them in the form above and return the verdict verbatim into the

## 7. AMENDMENT — the owner's method ruling (2026-11-23 ~00:00 EET, his direct word)

After rounds 1–3 (all `free/gpt-6-luna`, blind mix) returned VERDICT: NO — rounds 2 and 3
resting on a documented blind-sort artifact (the critic sorted this film's mayor-face
frames as the bar film's character, both films sharing the house suited-character design,
then faulted the face's absence) — the owner ruled, verbatim:

> «الاتنين: ناقد جديد + فصل الفرز عن الحكم»
> ("Both: a new critic + decoupling the sort from the verdict.")

Applied from round 4 onward:

- **(a) New critic, different model class.** Each round's critic comes from a different
  model class than the round before. Round 4's critic is **Gemini** (`gemini-3.5-flash`)
  via the desk's own API connector (`custom.gemini-api`). The critic is **fresh**: no
  build history and **no prior rounds' reports or verdicts are fed to it** (this suspends
  §1's "same moments + my last report" for round 4; freshness under the ruling governs).
- **(b) The blind sort is decoupled from the verdict.** A round now runs in two separate
  fresh calls:
  - **Step A — blind sort, SCORE ONLY.** The same blind mix (our frames + bar frames,
    same size, shuffled, neutral names; key held by the driver) is sorted by the critic.
    The driver key-checks the score from the critic's per-picture lines and records it
    (header-vs-lines discrepancies recorded honestly, as in rounds 1–3). The sort is a
    number in the record — **it can no longer produce, support, or contaminate a
    verdict**: a sort slip can never again invent a missing element.
  - **Step B — the verdict, judged directly.** In a separate fresh call the critic
    receives **our frames only, labelled as the film under review** (no bar frames),
    plus the pass exit spec (§2 standing BLOCKING checks + §3.2/§3.3 for the round's
    scope), and answers in the fixed form of §5 (minus the BLIND SORT line, which now
    lives in Step A). BIGGEST GAP / BLOCKING / VERDICT rest solely on what is visibly in
    our frames measured against the spec.
- **What stands unchanged (RULES §3):** a NEW critic every round; fix only the BIGGEST
  GAP, at its cause, one cause one change; a BLOCKING one-line fix may ride along; the
  pass is done only when its exit list is true AND the critic answers `VERDICT: YES`
  with no BLOCKING fault; the same fault twice means change the method, not the amount;
  the director's review never replaces critic rounds; the driver never overturns a
  critic — a verdict resting on a factual error about the frames is recorded precisely
  and reported, not silently corrected.

## 7.2 AMENDMENT — pack completeness rule (phase 8, from round 7's pack failure)

Round 7's pack supplied 12 frames while its own spec text (`prompt_stepB.txt`)
named 16 — frames 590, 639, 669, 711, 723, 768, 822 were judged in the spec but
never shown, and several BLOCKING items cited their absence. Recorded in full in
`CRITIC_REPORT_round7.md`; the verdict stood (the driver never overturns a
critic), but the mismatch was our pack-design fault, not a film fault.

**RULE for every future pack: a round's pack must supply every frame its spec
text names, or the spec text must be trimmed to the supplied frames.** The pack
builder checks the spec text's frame list against the supplied stills before the
pack is declared ready; a mismatch blocks the pack, not the film. The round-8
pack is the first built under this rule.
// AUTHORSHIP: DRIVER-AUTHORED (phase-9 driver, local agent, 2026-11-23) —
// NOT a Codex seat session. Pending Codex re-derivation per brief
// P9_PIXEL_GATE_REDERIVE_BRIEF.md; until the seat version lands this file is
// a driver harness, not seat-authored production code. (Guard question 04:46.)
// PIXEL GATE — fudai-wall method change #3 (phase 9, on critic round 8's
// demand + RULES S3: the same fault twice changes the METHOD).
// The scene-data staging audit (tools/audit_staging.mjs) stays as a PRE-CHECK.
// This gate judges the fleet-probed STILLS themselves and is the only staging
// verdict that counts: no frame is called staged until it passes HERE.
//   (a) HEAD CROP - the required face's RENDERED head disc is measured in the
//       still by tools/pixel_measure.py (skin segmentation ringed by the dark
//       outline), matched to the audit-projected centre/size, and fails if
//       the disc is cut by a frame edge or cannot be found unambiguously.
//   (b) FOREGROUND OCCLUSION - screen-space: a named prop's legibility rect
//       (projected from scene data) fails if a CLOSER character disc
//       (calibrated radius 1.35x the model sphere) or a closer prop box
//       covers >10% of it. The staging audit's occluder set (buildings +
//       wall + gateleaf) cannot see these; this test can.
//   (c) SET BAR - RULES S5 detail/flat percentages computed on the pixels
//       (32x32 blocks, grayscale std). S5 is a per-SHOT bar: a shot passes
//       when the MEDIAN of its key frames is >=22% detail and <=78% flat;
//       the film needs median shot >=28% detail and <=40% of shots under.
// Calibration (2026-11-23, chunk2d stills, recorded in lessons/build.md):
// rendered head radius = 1.04-1.06x the audit sphere for the mayor, ~1.32x
// hat-inclusive for villagers; rendered centres sit ~340-620 px ABOVE the
// audit projection on close shots - hence discs are measured, not projected.
// Usage: node tools/audit_pixels.mjs [--stills <dir>] [--frames 687,723]
// The geometry preamble mirrors tools/audit_staging.mjs (same pipeline:
// resolver/lowCams/autoMove sampled identically).
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { dirname, join } from 'node:path';
import film from '../scenes/film.js';
import { resolver, resolveTimes, autoMove } from '../lib/film/resolve.js';
import { easeFn } from '../engine/ease.js';
import { lowCams } from '../lib/film/end.js';

const ROOT = join(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..');
const args = process.argv.slice(2);
const opt = (k, d) => { const i = args.indexOf(k); return i >= 0 ? args[i + 1] : d; };
const STILLS = opt('--stills', join(ROOT, 'probes/chunk2d/stills'));
const ONLY = opt('--frames', null)?.split(',').map(Number);
const words = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(ROOT, 'audio/clean.json'), 'utf8'));
const lines = [...new Set(words.map(w => w.line))].map(line => {
  const ws = words.filter(w => w.line === line);
  return { line, start: ws[0].start, end: ws.at(-1).end };
});
const voice = { words, lines };
const R = resolver(voice);
let shots = resolveTimes(film.shots, R, 'shots');
const acts = Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(film.acts || {}).map(([id, a]) => [id, resolveTimes(a, R, `acts.${id}`)]));
const props = resolveTimes(film.props || [], R, 'props');
const dur = 68.08;
const cast = film.cast;
const characters = Object.entries(cast).map(([name, c]) => {
  const raw = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(ROOT, 'scenes', c.spec), 'utf8'));
  const spec = { ...raw, id: name };
  const legLen = spec.limbs?.legLen ?? 1.35;
  const hipY = legLen * .93;
  const rest = { hipY, headY: hipY + (spec.body?.neckHeight ?? .35) + (spec.body?.neckToHead ?? .35), neckToHead: spec.body?.neckToHead ?? .35, legLen, armLen: spec.limbs?.armLen ?? .95,
    ankleY: (spec.limbs?.width ?? .058) / 2 + .005, hipR: [-.22, -.2, 0], hipL: [.22, -.2, 0],
    shoulderR: [-.4, .4, 0], shoulderL: [.4, .4, 0], neck: [0, .4, 0] };
  const timeline = resolveTimes(JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(acts[name] || {})), R, `acts.${name}`);
  timeline.root = timeline.root || { x: c.at?.[0] ?? 0, z: c.at?.[1] ?? 0, ry: c.ry ?? 0 };
  timeline.settle ??= true;
  return { name, spec, timeline, rest };
});
const rests = Object.fromEntries(characters.map(ch => [ch.name, ch.rest]));
function posAt(id, t) {
  const c = cast[id], base = c.at, walks = (acts[id]?.walk || []).filter(w => t >= w.t0).sort((a,b)=>a.t0-b.t0);
  if (!walks.length) return [base[0], 0, base[1]];
  const w = walks.at(-1), path = w.path, speed = w.speed || 1;
  let remain = Math.max(0, t - w.t0) * speed;
  for (let i=1;i<path.length;i++) {
    const a=path[i-1], b=path[i], d=Math.hypot(b[0]-a[0],b[1]-a[1]);
    if (remain <= d) { const u=d ? remain/d:1; return [a[0]+(b[0]-a[0])*u,0,a[1]+(b[1]-a[1])*u]; }
    remain -= d;
  }
  const p=path.at(-1); return [p[0],0,p[1]];
}
const clamp=(x,a=0,b=1)=>Math.max(a,Math.min(b,x));
const lerp=(a,b,u)=>a+(b-a)*u;
function lerpV(a,b,u){return a.map((v,i)=>lerp(v,b[i],u));}
const lowered = lowCams(shots, characters, rests, { log: () => {} });
shots = lowered.shots;
const moved = await autoMove(shots, dur);
const shotById = id => moved.find(s => s.id === id);
function camAt(shot,t){const c=shot.camera,a=c.t0??shot.t0,b=c.t1??shot.t1;let u=clamp((t-a)/(b-a));u=easeFn(c.ease??'outCubic')(u);return {pos:lerpV(c.from.pos,c.to.pos,u),target:lerpV(c.from.target,c.to.target,u),fov:lerp(c.from.fov??26,c.to.fov??26,u)};}
function add(a,b){return a.map((x,i)=>x+b[i]);}
function ry(v,yaw){const a=yaw*Math.PI/180;return [v[0]*Math.cos(a)+v[2]*Math.sin(a),v[1],-v[0]*Math.sin(a)+v[2]*Math.cos(a)];}
function box(center,size,yaw=0){const [x,y,z]=size,a=Math.abs(yaw*Math.PI/180);const ex=Math.abs(x*Math.cos(a))+Math.abs(z*Math.sin(a)),ez=Math.abs(x*Math.sin(a))+Math.abs(z*Math.cos(a));return {center,min:[center[0]-ex/2,center[1]-y/2,center[2]-ez/2],max:[center[0]+ex/2,center[1]+y/2,center[2]+ez/2],size:[ex,y,ez]};}
function corners(b){const out=[];for(const x of [b.min[0],b.max[0]])for(const y of [b.min[1],b.max[1]])for(const z of [b.min[2],b.max[2]])out.push([x,y,z]);return out;}
function makeFrame(cam){const f=norm(sub(cam.target,cam.pos)), right=norm(cross(f,[0,1,0])), up=norm(cross(right,f));const v=cam.fov*Math.PI/180,h=2*Math.atan(Math.tan(v/2)*1080/1920);
  return {cam,depth(p){return dot(sub(p,cam.pos),f);},height(size,p){const d=this.depth(p);return d>0?2*Math.atan(size/(2*d))/(cam.fov*Math.PI/180)*1920:null;},screen(p){const d=sub(p,cam.pos),z=dot(d,f);if(z<=.1)return null;const x=dot(d,right)/(z*Math.tan(h/2)),y=dot(d,up)/(z*Math.tan(v/2));return [(x+1)*540,(1-y)*960];}};}
const sub=(a,b)=>a.map((v,i)=>v-b[i]), dot=(a,b)=>a.reduce((s,v,i)=>s+v*b[i],0), norm=a=>a.map(v=>v/(Math.hypot(...a)||1)), cross=(a,b)=>[a[1]*b[2]-a[2]*b[1],a[2]*b[0]-a[0]*b[2],a[0]*b[1]-a[1]*b[0]];
function propState(p,t){const ks=p.keys||[], before=ks.filter(k=>k.t<=t).sort((a,b)=>a.t-b.t), key=before.at(-1)||{};const lastField=f=>before.filter(k=>k[f]!=null).at(-1)?.[f];const visible=key.drop?true:(lastField('in')??p.in??'world')==='world';const at=[...(lastField('at')??p.at)],rot=[...(lastField('rot')??p.rot??[0,0,0])];
  const val=(field,def)=>{if(p[field]==null)return def;const keyed=before.filter(k=>k[field]!=null);if(!keyed.length)return p[field];const prev=keyed.at(-1),ix=keyed.length-1,ease=prev.ease;if(ease!=null&&ix>0){const prior=keyed[ix-1],u=clamp((t-prev.t)/ease);const sm=u*u*(3-2*u);return lerp(prior[field],prev[field],sm);}return prev[field];};
  return {visible,at,rot,page:val('page',0),h:val('h',.8),lift:val('lift',0),lineY:val('lineY',1),pin:val('pin',0),cx:val('cx',0),cy:val('cy',12)};}
function dropState(p,t){const k=(p.keys||[]).filter(k=>k.t<=t).at(-1);if(!k?.drop)return null;const d=k.drop,dt=t-k.t,g=d.g??9.8,v0=d.v0||[0,0,0],from=d.from||k.at||p.at,ground=d.ground??0,a=g/2,c=from[1]-ground,T=a>0?(v0[1]+Math.sqrt(v0[1]**2+4*a*Math.max(0,c)))/(2*a):Infinity;if(d.hide&&dt>T)return null;const u=Math.min(dt,T),y=from[1]+v0[1]*u-.5*g*u*u;return [from[0]+v0[0]*u,y,from[2]+v0[2]*u];}
function propBox(id,t){const p=props.find(x=>x.id===id);if(!p)return null;const s=propState(p,t);if(!s.visible)return null;const a=s.at,r=s.rot[1]||0,kind=p.kind;let B;
  if(kind==='boardsign')B=box(add(a,[0,(p.post?p.leg||1.2:0)+(p.h||.8)/2,0]),[p.w||2,p.h||.8,.12],r);
  if(kind==='calendar')B=box(add(a,[0,1.9,0]),[2.2,3.5,.2],r);
  if(kind==='money')B=box(add(a,[0,.55,0]),[3.7,1.15,2.1],r);
  if(kind==='crane')B=box(add(a,ry([2,8,0],r)),[11.5,16.8,1.2],r);
  if(kind==='gateleaf')B=box(add(a,[0,17.88*(s.lift-.5),0]),[7,17.88,.36],r);
  if(kind==='seawall')B=box(add(a,[0,9,0]),[90,18,1.15],r);
  if(kind==='blueprint')B=box(add(a,[0,1.7,0]),[3.4,3.4,.2],r);
  if(kind==='orb')return null;
  if(kind==='coins'){const q=dropState(p,t);return q?{box:box(q,[.5,.5,.5]),state:s}:null;}
  return B?{box:B,state:s}:null;
}
const moments=[
 [219,7.3,'SH04'],[241,8.0333333333,'SH04'],[288,9.6,'SH05'],[345,11.5,'SH05'],[414,13.8,'SH05'],[474,15.8,'SH06'],[537,17.9,'SH07'],[570,19,'SH07'],[590,19.6666667,'SH07B'],[621,20.7,'SH08'],[639,21.3,'SH08'],[669,22.3,'SH09'],[687,22.9,'SH09'],[711,23.7,'SH10'],[723,24.1,'SH10'],[768,25.6,'SH11'],[798,26.6,'SH11'],[822,27.4,'SH11'],[870,29,'SH12']
